=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given sequences of positive integers of length N: A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N) and B=(B_1,B_2,\ldots,B_N).
You are given Q queries to process in order. The i-th query is explained below.

- You are given positive integers l_i,r_i,L_i,R_i. Print Yes if it is possible to rearrange the subsequence (A_{l_i},A_{l_i+1},\ldots,A_{r_i}) to match the subsequence (B_{L_i},B_{L_i+1},\ldots,B_{R_i}), and No otherwise.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N Q
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N
B_1 B_2 \ldots B_N
l_1 r_1 L_1 R_1
l_2 r_2 L_2 R_2
\vdots
l_Q r_Q L_Q R_Q

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th query.

Constraints


-  1\leq N,Q\leq 2\times 10^5
-  1\leq A_i,B_i\leq N
-  1\leq l_i \leq r_i\leq N
-  1\leq L_i \leq R_i\leq N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 4
1 2 3 2 4
2 3 1 4 2
1 3 1 3
1 2 3 5
1 4 2 5
1 5 1 5

Sample Output 1

Yes
No
No
Yes


- For the 1st query, it is possible to rearrange (1,2,3) to match (2,3,1). Hence, we print Yes.
- For the 2nd query, it is impossible to rearrange (1,2) in any way to match (1,4,2). Hence, we print No.
- For the 3rd query, it is impossible to rearrange (1,2,3,2) in any way to match (3,1,4,2). Hence, we print No.
- For the 4th query, it is possible to rearrange (1,2,3,2,4) to match (2,3,1,4,2). Hence, we print Yes.

Sample Input 2

4 4
4 4 4 4
4 4 4 4
1 2 2 3
3 3 1 1
1 3 1 4
1 4 2 3

Sample Output 2

Yes
Yes
No
No

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

If not e_old and e_new: bad -=1

Need maybe use "mod 2^64" with `& MASK`; but if using two 64-bit, prefix arrays of Python ints. Let's write code:

import random, sys
def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N,Q = map(int,input().split())
    A = list(map(int,input().split()))
    B = list(map(int,input().split()))
    MASK = (1<<64)-1
    # generate random weights
    w1 = [0]*(N+1)
    w2 = [0]*(N+1)
    # maybe use random.getrandbits
    rng = random.Random(123456789) # deterministic? Hmm.
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        w1[i] = rng.getrandbits(64)
        w2[i] = rng.getrandbits(64)
    prefA1 = [0]*(N+1)
    prefA2 = [0]*(N+1)
    prefB1 = [0]*(N+1)
    prefB2 = [0]*(N+1)
    for i,x in enumerate(A,1):
        prefA1[i] = (prefA1[i-1] + w1[x]) & MASK
        prefA2[i] = (prefA2[i-1] + w2[x]) & MASK
    for i,x in enumerate(B,1):
        prefB1[i] = (prefB1[i-1] + w1[x]) & MASK
        prefB2[i] = (prefB2[i-1] + w2[x]) & MASK
    out=[]
    for _ in range(Q):
        l,r,L,R = map(int,input().split())
        if r-l != R-L:
            out.append("No")
            continue
        ha1 = (prefA1[r] - prefA1[l-1]) & MASK
        hb1 = (prefB1[R] - prefB1[L-1]) & MASK
        if ha1 != hb1:
            out.append("No"); continue
        ha2 = (prefA2[r] - prefA2[l-1]) & MASK
        hb2 = (prefB2[R] - prefB2[L-1]) & MASK
        out.append("Yes" if ha2 == hb2 else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

Pseudo:
import sys, random
def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N,Q = map(int,input().split())
    MASK = (1<<128)-1
    rng = random.Random()  # or random module
    w = [0]*(N+1)
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        w[i] = rng.getrandbits(128)
    prefA = [0]*(N+1)
    for i, x in enumerate(map(int, input().split()), 1):
        prefA[i] = (prefA[i-1] + w[x]) & MASK
    prefB = [0]*(N+1)
    for i, x in enumerate(map(int, input().split()), 1):
        prefB[i] = (prefB[i-1] + w[x]) & MASK
    del w
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        l,r,L,R = map(int,input().split())
        if r - l != R - L:
            out.append("No")
        else:
            ha = (prefA[r] - prefA[l-1]) & MASK
            hb = (prefB[R] - prefB[L-1]) & MASK
            out.append("Yes" if ha == hb else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
if __name__ == "__main__": solve()

Let's maybe implement a deterministic splitmix64-based 128-bit hash with a random seed from `time`? Actually we can generate weights using splitmix64 with a random seed:

def splitmix64(x):
    x = (x + 0x9e3779b97f4a7c15) & MASK64
    x = (x ^ (x >> 30)) * 0xbf58476d1ce4e5b9 & MASK64
    x = (x ^ (x >> 27)) * 0x94d049bb133111eb & MASK64
    return x ^ (x >> 31)
w[i] = (splitmix64(seed + i) << 64) | splitmix64(seed + i + N)

Let's maybe write final code:

```python
import sys
import random

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, Q = map(int, input().split())
    MASK = (1 << 128) - 1

    rng = random.Random()
    w = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        w[i] = rng.getrandbits(128)

    prefA = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i, x in enumerate(map(int, input().split()), 1):
        prefA[i] = (prefA[i - 1] + w[x]) & MASK

    prefB = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i, x in enumerate(map(int, input().split()), 1):
        prefB[i] = (prefB[i - 1] + w[x]) & MASK

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        l, r, L, R = map(int, input().split())
        if r - l != R - L:
            out.append("No")
            continue
        ha = (prefA[r] - prefA[l - 1]) & MASK
        hb = (prefB[R] - prefB[L - 1]) & MASK
        out.append("Yes" if ha == hb else "No")

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```

This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "prefA" and "prefB" if N=2e5 and values are up to N, but if input has value N and w list length N+1, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the sum of weights for a value appearing c times is c*w, but if c is large and w has low bits zero, the contribution may be zero modulo 2^128 if c*w = 0 mod 2^128. This would mean that value is invisible. Probability for a given value and count c: P(c*w ≡0 mod 2^128) = 2^{v2(c)}/2^128? If c has v2=k, probability 2^{-(128-k)}. For c up to 2e5, k<=17, so <=2^-111. Union over values and queries ~2e5*2e5*2^-111? Wait for each query and each value, if that value is the only mismatch? But collision event requires total sum zero. The probability that a particular value's contribution is zero is small. The union bound over all queries and values: Q*N*2^-111 ~ 4e10*2^-111 ~2^-83. Still fine. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "using 128-bit hash" and "prefix sums" if the random weights are generated with `random.getrandbits(128)` but Python's random uses 53-bit precision? No, getrandbits arbitrary.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "No
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
import random

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, Q = map(int, input().split())

    MASK = (1 << 128) - 1
    rng = random.Random()

    weight = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        weight[i] = rng.getrandbits(128)

    prefA = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i, x in enumerate(map(int, input().split()), 1):
        prefA[i] = (prefA[i - 1] + weight[x]) & MASK

    prefB = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i, x in enumerate(map(int, input().split()), 1):
        prefB[i] = (prefB[i - 1] + weight[x]) & MASK

    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        l, r, L, R = map(int, input().split())

        if r - l != R - L:
            out.append("No")
            continue

        ha = (prefA[r] - prefA[l - 1]) & MASK
        hb = (prefB[R] - prefB[L - 1]) & MASK
        out.append("Yes" if ha == hb else "No")

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
